# MASTER CV — EVIDENCE MAP

## Purpose

This document is the Phase 6A analytical bridge between the career database and the Master CV blueprint. It does **not** select final CV content. It maps documented database evidence to the Master CV sections it can potentially support, identifies the competency/evidence contribution, and preserves the exact database record IDs for traceability.

**Source of truth:** current career database snapshot (`dev.db`): 20 Experiences, 40 Projects, 163 Activities. No facts have been added beyond the database records.

## How to read this map

- **Potential CV destination** = where the evidence could be used in the Master CV; it is not a 6B inclusion decision.
- **Evidence contribution** = the engineering/academic/leadership signal directly supported by the record.
- **Activity evidence** retains the database's `What`, `Responsibility`, and `Result` so later drafting can trace claims back to source records.
- **R&D** follows the database's `isRnD` field.
- Activity/project tags are reproduced from the database; they are not newly invented competencies.

## Master CV section key

1. Professional Profile
2. Core Competencies
3. Professional Experience
4. Selected Technical / R&D Projects
5. Education
6. Research & Publications
7. Teaching & Academic Experience
8. Licences & Certifications
9. Technical Skills
10. Languages

## 2. Project-level evidence map

The following is the core 6A mapping. Every project is retained, but its role in the Master CV is distinguished from the later 6B decision about whether it receives space.

### Middle East Technical University — Undergraduate Student (`cmspt4fbh0000vje5am27bszx`)
#### Numerical Methods for Aerospace Engineers  (`cmsptl7zf0004vje5b6evxppt`)

- **Database R&D flag:** No
- **System:** Numerical solver implementations — unstructured FVM (potential flow), 1D FDM (parabolic/hyperbolic PDEs), 2D FDM (convection-diffusion)
- **Objective:** Implement and validate numerical solution schemes for aerospace-relevant governing equations, evaluate scheme stability and convergence, and visualize flow field results.
- **Outcome:** Three functional FORTRAN solvers delivered, covering FVM potential flow over a NACA 0012 airfoil and explicit/implicit FDM solutions for heat diffusion and linear convection-diffusion with parametric validation
- **Potential CV destination:** Education / Academic Experience; Core Competencies / Technical Skills
- **Evidence contribution:** Technical evidence: Systems Integration; Verification & Validation; Software / Digital Systems; Aerodynamics / Simulation.
- **Database project tags:** Implementation, Verification, FORTRAN, MATLAB, Aerodynamics, CFD (Computational Fluid Dynamics), Simulation, Numerical Methods, Software

| Activity | Responsibility | What | Result | Potential CV use |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `cmsptn4yc0006vje5wwbmcvw4` | Led | Developed a 2D finite volume solver in FORTRAN to compute potential flow over a NACA 0012 airfoil and a circle at multiple angles of attack. | Converged potential flow solutions obtained for both geometries; velocity vector and streamline distributions validated against expected inviscid flow behaviour at all three angles of attack. | Core professional evidence |
| `cmsptolqq0008vje58rjlmt8v` | Led | Implemented and compared explicit and implicit 1D finite difference schemes for an unsteady heat diffusion equation (parabolic PDE) and a linear convection equation (hyperbolic PDE). | Confirmed FTCS stability limit (d < 0.5) for heat diffusion; demonstrated unconditional stability of BTCS; identified numerical diffusion in FTBS for the wave equation. Results consistent with theoretical Von Neumann stability analysis. | Core professional evidence |
| `cmsptpyva000avje5f2ngf1ks` | Led | Implemented FTCS and DuFort-Frankel schemes to solve the 2D unsteady convection-diffusion equation over a rectangular domain, with parametric studies on grid resolution, convection velocity, and initial condition geometry | Demonstrated conditional stability of FTCS and improved stability characteristics of DuFort-Frankel; confirmed numerical diffusion effects under backward convective differencing; parametric cases validated solver response to varying flow and grid conditions. | Core professional evidence |

#### Finite Element Modelling of a Composite Cantilever Beam (`cmspu3quh000dvje5d5n2g2eq`)

- **Database R&D flag:** No
- **System:** Composite Cantilever Beam — Aluminium/Glass Fibre Epoxy Layered Structure
- **Objective:** Determine tip deflection, stress distribution, torsional frequencies, and angle of twist of a multi-layer composite cantilever beam under static and dynamic loading
- **Outcome:** Delivered two complete FEA reports covering static structural analysis (δ_max = 31.387mm under 5N tip load) and torsional analysis (δ_max = 2.296mm static, θ_max = 5.74×10⁻⁴ rad/mm under 1Nm moment), with modal frequency extraction and harmonic response at 1Hz and 144Hz.
- **Potential CV destination:** Education / Academic Experience; Core Competencies / Technical Skills
- **Evidence contribution:** Technical evidence: Systems Integration; Verification & Validation; Software / Digital Systems; Aerodynamics / Simulation.
- **Database project tags:** Design, Verification, ANSYS Mechanical APDL, Software, Simulation, Structural Analysis, Structures

| Activity | Responsibility | What | Result | Potential CV use |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `cmspu55yo000fvje5vsg3rzev` | IndependentlyExecuted | Performed static 3D FEA of a 5-layer composite cantilever beam (3 aluminium + 2 glass fibre/epoxy, 500mm × 50mm) under a 5N transverse tip load to determine tip deflection and full stress distribution across all layers. | Maximum tip deflection δ_max = 31.387mm. Dominant stress component identified as σ_x (max ±0.45×10⁸ N/m²) concentrated at the cantilevered root on the top and bottom surfaces. XZ shear stress dominant across the beam body (avg 58,374 N/m²). Results consistent with classical cantilever beam theory. | Core professional evidence |
| `cmspu6fn3000hvje58n5i6sc5` | IndependentlyExecuted | Performed multi-analysis 3D FEA of a 3-layer composite cantilever beam (1 aluminium core + 2 glass fibre/epoxy, 200mm × 40mm) under static and harmonic torsional moment loading (Mx = 1Nm), including modal frequency extraction and harmonic response analysis. | Static: δ_max = 2.296mm, θ_max = 5.74×10⁻⁴ rad/mm. First three torsional frequencies identified at 288.275Hz (mode 3), 894.912Hz (mode 6), and 1586.38Hz (mode 8). Harmonic at 1Hz matched static results; harmonic at 144Hz showed dynamic amplification (δ_max = 2.923mm, θ_max = 7.3075×10⁻⁴ rad/mm). Tip displacement confirmed to vary linearly across beam width in all cases | Core professional evidence |

#### Turbofan Compressor Performance Analysis (`cmspud4l8000jvje5y3nu63dd`)

- **Database R&D flag:** Yes
- **System:** Turbofan Engine Compressor — Corrected Performance Parameter Model (bypass ratio α=2.82, design point π_c=24, M_0=0.6)
- **Objective:** Determine how turbine inlet temperature, operating altitude, and turbine inlet area influence compressor pressure ratio and corrected mass flow rate, and identify operating limits relative to compressor map constraints.
- **Outcome:** Produced parametric performance plots for corrected mass flow rate and compressor pressure ratio across T_t4 range (1000–2000K) and altitude range (sea level to 15,000m), demonstrating throttle, altitude, and geometry effects on compressor operating point.
- **Potential CV destination:** Education / Academic Experience; Selected Technical / R&D Projects; Core Competencies / Technical Skills
- **Evidence contribution:** R&D / experimental technical evidence.
- **Database project tags:** Design, Software, Propulsion, Simulation, MATLAB

| Activity | Responsibility | What | Result | Potential CV use |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `cmspuei9z000lvje5i6aiki9v` | Contributed | Conducted parametric analysis of turbofan compressor performance by modelling the effects of turbine inlet temperature, operating altitude, and turbine inlet area on corrected mass flow rate and compressor pressure ratio | Demonstrated that increasing T_t4 raises compressor pressure ratio for a given mass flow rate; confirmed θ_0 reduction with altitude and its attenuation near tropopause; showed that increasing A_4 beyond choked-flow area reduces turbine energy extraction and consequently drops compressor mass flow rate. All results presented as parametric families of curves against compressor map limits. | Core professional evidence |

#### Finite Difference Method Solvers for Aerodynamic PDEs (`cmspukif6000nvje5yi63tczi`)

- **Database R&D flag:** No
- **System:** MATLAB FDM Solver Suite — 2D Laplace (100×100 grid), 1D Heat Equation (FTCS explicit), 1D Wave Equation (Lax-Wendroff and MacCormack)
- **Objective:** Implement stable FDM solvers for four PDE classes, conduct Von Neumann stability analysis, enforce stability limits, and demonstrate convergent and divergent behaviour across parametric Courant and diffusion number sweeps over a 10-second simulation window.
- **Outcome:** Delivered four functional MATLAB solvers with convergence-verified results; confirmed FTCS heat equation stability limit (λ < 0.5); demonstrated wave propagation fidelity and instability onset (ν > 1) for both Lax-Wendroff and MacCormack schemes; Laplace solver converged to steady-state heat distribution in 1,451 iterations on a 100×100 grid.
- **Potential CV destination:** Education / Academic Experience; Core Competencies / Technical Skills
- **Evidence contribution:** Technical evidence: Systems Integration; Verification & Validation; Software / Digital Systems; Aerodynamics / Simulation.
- **Database project tags:** Implementation, Verification, MATLAB, Aerodynamics, CFD (Computational Fluid Dynamics), Numerical Methods, Simulation, Software

| Activity | Responsibility | What | Result | Potential CV use |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `cmspulziq000pvje59nizuskl` | Led | Led development of four MATLAB finite difference solvers covering elliptic, parabolic, and hyperbolic PDE classes, applying ASE301 numerical methods experience to architect the solution approach and implement stability-verified codes. | All four solvers delivered converged results within stability limits. Laplace solution reached steady-state at iteration 1,451. Heat equation confirmed theoretical λ < 0.5 stability bound with divergence visually demonstrated at λ = 1. Both wave equation schemes produced clean sinusoidal propagation at ν ≤ 1 and catastrophic divergence at ν = 1.33. MacCormack showed reduced numerical diffusion compared to Lax-Wendroff at equivalent ν values. | Core professional evidence |

#### Simulation of a piezoelectric d15 torsion sensor (`cmspy2tko001svje59wpjbjda`)

- **Database R&D flag:** Yes
- **System:** Smart Composite Structure — Cantilevered Piezoelectric d₁₅ Torsion Sensor (FEA Simulation)
- **Objective:** Determine the sensor voltage generated by a PIC255 piezoceramic d₁₅ torsion sensor under 1 Nm torsional moment loading at quasi-static (1Hz) and dynamic (first torsional frequency) conditions, and compute the electromechanical coupling coefficient across three torsional modes
- **Outcome:** Quasi-static sensor voltage of 1.789 V obtained at 1Hz; harmonic sensor voltage of 2.31 V at 55.895 Hz. First three torsional frequencies extracted at 111.79 Hz, 414.71 Hz, and 885.42 Hz. Electromechanical coupling coefficients computed as k = 0.0073, 0.0076, and 0.0026 for modes 2, 4, and 7 respectively.
- **Potential CV destination:** Education / Academic Experience; Selected Technical / R&D Projects; Research & Publications; Core Competencies / Technical Skills
- **Evidence contribution:** R&D / experimental technical evidence.
- **Database project tags:** Design, Validation, Sensors, Research, Simulation, Technical Documentation, Numerical Methods, Structural Analysis, Abaqus CAE

|---|---|---|---|---|

| `cmspy67x7001wvje5npg4n59n` | IndependentlyExecuted | Built the full Abaqus CAE finite element model of the cantilevered piezoelectric beam including geometry, boundary conditions, constraints, and mesh. | Fully meshed and constrained Abaqus CAE model with correct piezoelectric element type, material orientations, and boundary conditions — ready for simulation | Research / R&D evidence |
| `cmspy7uew001yvje5qkfr07ms` | IndependentlyExecuted | Executed three sequential FEA analyses — quasi-static, modal, and harmonic — to extract sensor voltages and electromechanical coupling coefficients across torsional modes. | Quasi-static sensor voltage = 1.789 V at 1 Hz. - **Objective:** Model the 3D aerodynamic flow field of a multirotor in low-rate axial descent, calculate individual rotor thrust and drag torque via Blade Element Theory, and evaluate tip vortex ring state interactions.
- **Outcome:** Generated computational background domains and refined rotor disk meshes, executed parallel 4-processor steady-state solves in simpleFoam, achieved residual convergence within 410 iterations, and validated axial pressure distribution jumps and non-interacting tip vortex behavior against analytical momentum theory

## 4. Cross-cutting competency evidence

This section identifies where the database provides evidence for the competencies already established in Phase 5. It does **not** assign new proficiency levels.

### Systems Engineering
- **Amazilia Aerospace GmbH — WfA MiniFreighter GCS -SN2** / `cmsl0gu6m0003vj7zt3rupt43` — Attended joint design evaluation session with Wings for Aid CEO and chief pilot alongside Amazilia CTO and senior systems engineer. Captured customer and pilot requirements directly and translated them into formal engineering specifications for the SN2 GCS redesign. Produced wiring diagrams, CAD renders and system architecture documentation. Presented across three internal design reviews with CTO, Head of Systems Engineering and Senior Systems Engineer, incorporating embedded software team input on DCU interface changes. Received sign-off to proceed to BOM and procurement. → Approved SN2 GCS design package including updated wiring architecture, component specifications and BOM — cleared for procurement and assembly
- **Amazilia Aerospace GmbH — WfA MiniFreighter GCS -SN2** / `cmsl0tq880005vj7zubflr85l` — Sourced alternative panel supplier through independent research, which reduced component costs compared to the original source. Redesigned front panels in Autodesk Inventor to match the new supplier's specifications. Curated a full assembly procedure based on SN1 documentation and discussions with seniors. Upon parts arrival, performed mechanical assembly including drilling connection holes on panels and GCS case, installing internal support structures, and routing the power architecture — standardising all internal components on 12VDC via an AC/DC converter. → Completed mechanical assembly and power architecture of SN2 Unit 1, with reduced component cost versus SN1 baseline
- **Amazilia Aerospace GmbH — WfA MiniFreighter GCS -SN2** / `cmsl0xr2q0007vj7zz7lho71h` — Performed stepwise electrical integration of all internal GCS components following the assembly procedure, conducting QA checks and functionality tests at marked checkpoints verified with seniors. Installed GCS software received from the software team upon completion of Unit 1. Scheduled and conducted simulator testing in the lab to evaluate control software, internal wiring of controls, stick feedback and aircraft response time. Applied personal drone and private pilot experience to assess pilot feel and control responsiveness directly during simulation runs. Coordinated firmware and wiring corrections on the hardware side while communicating required software changes to the software team. Conducted a final internal review before inviting the Wings for Aid CEO and chief pilot for customer acceptance testing against the original specifications. → Unit 1 passed all internal tests and customer acceptance checks. Customer confirmed the GCS met specifications. Cleared for serial production of remaining four units
- **Amazilia Aerospace GmbH — WfA MiniFreighter GCS -SN2** / `cmsl109pv0009vj7zg67jlvc0` — Serially produced four additional GCS units following customer acceptance of Unit 1. Worked on all four units simultaneously in a dedicated assembly space, applying the verified assembly procedure and QA process established during Unit 1 production. Each unit went through the same integration, functionality testing and verification process before being cleared for delivery → Five GCS units total completed, tested and delivered to Wings for Aid for operational deployment with WFP in Africa
- **Amazilia Aerospace GmbH — Amazilia Ground Control Station** / `cmslpoi510003vjtdd6ggwe7u` — Reviewed and cleaned up existing CAD files, wiring diagrams and system architecture documentation inherited from predecessor. - **Amazilia Aerospace GmbH — Aircraft Battery Charging Unit (ABCU)** / `cmslym8dk0003vjtk9jmmadj6` — Following a polarity reversal incident during field testing of a Pipistrel aircraft that caused a short circuit and wire damage, received brief from CTO to design a safe standalone aircraft battery charging unit. Conducted requirements capture by consulting all field testing personnel to understand operational needs, environmental conditions and safety requirements. Researched LiFePO4 battery chemistry, charging characteristics and available charging system suppliers to establish a technically grounded requirements baseline. → Clear requirements baseline established covering dual-voltage capability (12V and 24V LiFePO4), polarity protection, voltage indication, field deployability and safe external charging operation
- **Amazilia Aerospace GmbH — Aircraft Battery Charging Unit (ABCU)** / `cmslynjpz0005vjtko4xpmzxv` — Developed system architecture for the ABCU covering power input, charging system selection, voltage switching between 12V and 24V LiFePO4 configurations, polarity protection, battery voltage indication and enclosure design. Conducted detailed component market study matching available charging systems against Amazilia's battery specifications. Selected components capable of handling both 12V and 24V LiFePO4 chemistries with switchable output, allowing field teams to switch between configurations during operations. Optimised component selection for cost following design review feedback. → Approved system architecture and component selection covering dual-voltage charging capability with polarity protection and field-deployable form factor
- **Amazilia Aerospace GmbH — Aircraft Battery Charging Unit (ABCU)** / `cmslyp38c0007vjtkk48fnbh7` — Produced full wiring diagrams for the ABCU internal architecture. - **HORYZN  — Kolibri eVTOL — Aerodynamics Module Development** / `cmsm3ixrw000svj6yk0weq016` — Participated in structured handover sessions with the outgoing Mission Pulse Phase 1 team to understand the design, shortcomings and failure modes of the Frankenstein prototype. Analysed the previous CPACS files, MATLAB code and PAWAT outputs to reconstruct what had been built and why transition had failed. Collaborated across the full Design Loop team to document lessons learned and define requirements list for the Phase 2 prototype — Kolibri. Requirements captured and managed in Notion. → Comprehensive requirements list established for Kolibri Phase 2, identifying transition failure as the primary design constraint to resolve
- **HORYZN  — Kolibri eVTOL — Aerodynamics Module Development** / `cmsm3lgib000uvj6yzkjd47i1` — Led the aerodynamics team's contribution to the Kolibri configuration selection process. Identified the root cause of Frankenstein's transition failure as wingtip-mounted propellers creating adverse interference at the 25kg MTOW scale. Proposed a Cessna-type fixed-wing configuration with a front tractor propeller and lift motors mounted on the wing, supported by case studies from undergraduate aircraft design projects and CFD analysis in OpenFOAM demonstrating adequate glide performance during transition in the event of propulsion switching delays. Presented the configuration argument across multiple design reviews, convincing the team and securing agreement to proceed. → Cessna-type fixed-wing hybrid lift-and-cruise configuration adopted for Kolibri, directly resolving the transition failure mode of Frankenstein
- **HORYZN  — Kolibri eVTOL — Aerodynamics Module Development** / `cmsm3npre000wvj6yr6elrnaw` — Developed and maintained the aerodynamics module within the HORYZN multidisciplinary design loop. The module interfaced with CPACS via TiGL and TiXi libraries, using PAWAT as the core aerodynamic solver within a MATLAB class-based architecture. Implemented methods covering airfoil database management, CPACS geometry parsing, aerodynamic mapping across Reynolds number and angle of attack ranges, twist optimisation, stall velocity and force calculations, neutral point calculation and results writing back to CPACS. Wrote full module documentation including README, UML class diagram, function descriptions and I/O specifications. Managed module codebase on Git. → Fully documented functional aerodynamics module integrated into the HORYZN RCE design loop, delivering aerodynamic coefficients, forces, moments and aeromap data to downstream modules via CPACS
- **HORYZN  — Kolibri eVTOL — Aerodynamics Module Development** / `cmsm3qzd2000yvj6ya3kpt1f7` — Participated in the aerodynamics team's airfoil selection process for Kolibri's lifting surfaces. Ran systematic case studies across candidate airfoils using XFLR5 and Flow5 to evaluate CL/CD performance across the expected Reynolds number range. Fed selected airfoil profiles into the CPACS aircraft definition and ran iterative optimisation of wing geometry including twist distribution, span, chord and sweep through the aerodynamics module. Coordinated with structures and geometry sub-teams within the design loop to ensure aerodynamic outputs were compatible with structural and sizing constraints. → Optimised lifting surface definition for Kolibri delivered to the design loop, meeting CL/CD, wing loading and stall requirements within structural and geometric constraints
- **HORYZN  — Kolibri eVTOL — Aerodynamics Module Development** / `cmsm3thgg0010vj6y72uhz100` — Worked intensively within the RCE-based multidisciplinary design loop alongside aerodynamics, structures, geometry, wing sizing and propulsion sub-teams to achieve design convergence for Kolibri. - **Middle East Technical University — Turbofan Compressor Performance Analysis** / `cmspuei9z000lvje5i6aiki9v` — Conducted parametric analysis of turbofan compressor performance by modelling the effects of turbine inlet temperature, operating altitude, and turbine inlet area on corrected mass flow rate and compressor pressure ratio → Demonstrated that increasing T_t4 raises compressor pressure ratio for a given mass flow rate; confirmed θ_0 reduction with altitude and its attenuation near tropopause; showed that increasing A_4 beyond choked-flow area reduces turbine energy extraction and consequently drops compressor mass flow rate. - **Middle East Technical University — Simulation of a piezoelectric d15 torsion sensor** / `cmspy7uew001yvje5qkfr07ms` — Executed three sequential FEA analyses — quasi-static, modal, and harmonic — to extract sensor voltages and electromechanical coupling coefficients across torsional modes. → Quasi-static sensor voltage = 1.789 V at 1 Hz. ### Systems Integration
- **Amazilia Aerospace GmbH — WfA MiniFreighter GCS -SN2** / `cmsl0gu6m0003vj7zt3rupt43` — Attended joint design evaluation session with Wings for Aid CEO and chief pilot alongside Amazilia CTO and senior systems engineer. Upon parts arrival, performed mechanical assembly including drilling connection holes on panels and GCS case, installing internal support structures, and routing the power architecture — standardising all internal components on 12VDC via an AC/DC converter. → Completed mechanical assembly and power architecture of SN2 Unit 1, with reduced component cost versus SN1 baseline
- **Amazilia Aerospace GmbH — WfA MiniFreighter GCS -SN2** / `cmsl0xr2q0007vj7zz7lho71h` — Performed stepwise electrical integration of all internal GCS components following the assembly procedure, conducting QA checks and functionality tests at marked checkpoints verified with seniors. Applied personal drone and private pilot experience to assess pilot feel and control responsiveness directly during simulation runs. Coordinated firmware and wiring corrections on the hardware side while communicating required software changes to the software team. Conducted a final internal review before inviting the Wings for Aid CEO and chief pilot for customer acceptance testing against the original specifications. → Unit 1 passed all internal tests and customer acceptance checks. Customer confirmed the GCS met specifications. Cleared for serial production of remaining four units
- **Amazilia Aerospace GmbH — WfA MiniFreighter GCS -SN2** / `cmsl109pv0009vj7zg67jlvc0` — Serially produced four additional GCS units following customer acceptance of Unit 1. - **Amazilia Aerospace GmbH — Aircraft Battery Charging Unit (ABCU)** / `cmslym8dk0003vjtk9jmmadj6` — Following a polarity reversal incident during field testing of a Pipistrel aircraft that caused a short circuit and wire damage, received brief from CTO to design a safe standalone aircraft battery charging unit. Researched LiFePO4 battery chemistry, charging characteristics and available charging system suppliers to establish a technically grounded requirements baseline. → Clear requirements baseline established covering dual-voltage capability (12V and 24V LiFePO4), polarity protection, voltage indication, field deployability and safe external charging operation
- **Amazilia Aerospace GmbH — Aircraft Battery Charging Unit (ABCU)** / `cmslynjpz0005vjtko4xpmzxv` — Developed system architecture for the ABCU covering power input, charging system selection, voltage switching between 12V and 24V LiFePO4 configurations, polarity protection, battery voltage indication and enclosure design. Selected components capable of handling both 12V and 24V LiFePO4 chemistries with switchable output, allowing field teams to switch between configurations during operations. Optimised component selection for cost following design review feedback. → Approved system architecture and component selection covering dual-voltage charging capability with polarity protection and field-deployable form factor
- **Amazilia Aerospace GmbH — Aircraft Battery Charging Unit (ABCU)** / `cmslyp38c0007vjtkk48fnbh7` — Produced full wiring diagrams for the ABCU internal architecture. Agreed final panel supplier — same alternative supplier used for the WfA GCS — following design review approval. → Fully approved detailed design package including wiring diagrams, enclosure CAD, panel designs, component specifications and BOM
- **Amazilia Aerospace GmbH — Aircraft Battery Charging Unit (ABCU)** / `cmslyqd5m0009vjtk5r8vkz24` — Upon departure from Amazilia, compiled and handed over the complete ABCU design package to the team. - **HORYZN  — Kolibri eVTOL — Aerodynamics Module Development** / `cmsm3ixrw000svj6yk0weq016` — Participated in structured handover sessions with the outgoing Mission Pulse Phase 1 team to understand the design, shortcomings and failure modes of the Frankenstein prototype. Analysed the previous CPACS files, MATLAB code and PAWAT outputs to reconstruct what had been built and why transition had failed. Collaborated across the full Design Loop team to document lessons learned and define requirements list for the Phase 2 prototype — Kolibri. Requirements captured and managed in Notion. → Comprehensive requirements list established for Kolibri Phase 2, identifying transition failure as the primary design constraint to resolve
- **HORYZN  — Kolibri eVTOL — Aerodynamics Module Development** / `cmsm3lgib000uvj6yzkjd47i1` — Led the aerodynamics team's contribution to the Kolibri configuration selection process. Identified the root cause of Frankenstein's transition failure as wingtip-mounted propellers creating adverse interference at the 25kg MTOW scale. Proposed a Cessna-type fixed-wing configuration with a front tractor propeller and lift motors mounted on the wing, supported by case studies from undergraduate aircraft design projects and CFD analysis in OpenFOAM demonstrating adequate glide performance during transition in the event of propulsion switching delays. Presented the configuration argument across multiple design reviews, convincing the team and securing agreement to proceed. → Cessna-type fixed-wing hybrid lift-and-cruise configuration adopted for Kolibri, directly resolving the transition failure mode of Frankenstein
- **HORYZN  — Kolibri eVTOL — Aerodynamics Module Development** / `cmsm3npre000wvj6yr6elrnaw` — Developed and maintained the aerodynamics module within the HORYZN multidisciplinary design loop. The module interfaced with CPACS via TiGL and TiXi libraries, using PAWAT as the core aerodynamic solver within a MATLAB class-based architecture. Implemented methods covering airfoil database management, CPACS geometry parsing, aerodynamic mapping across Reynolds number and angle of attack ranges, twist optimisation, stall velocity and force calculations, neutral point calculation and results writing back to CPACS. Wrote full module documentation including README, UML class diagram, function descriptions and I/O specifications. Managed module codebase on Git. → Fully documented functional aerodynamics module integrated into the HORYZN RCE design loop, delivering aerodynamic coefficients, forces, moments and aeromap data to downstream modules via CPACS
- **HORYZN  — Kolibri eVTOL — Aerodynamics Module Development** / `cmsm3qzd2000yvj6ya3kpt1f7` — Participated in the aerodynamics team's airfoil selection process for Kolibri's lifting surfaces. Ran systematic case studies across candidate airfoils using XFLR5 and Flow5 to evaluate CL/CD performance across the expected Reynolds number range. Fed selected airfoil profiles into the CPACS aircraft definition and ran iterative optimisation of wing geometry including twist distribution, span, chord and sweep through the aerodynamics module. Coordinated with structures and geometry sub-teams within the design loop to ensure aerodynamic outputs were compatible with structural and sizing constraints. → Optimised lifting surface definition for Kolibri delivered to the design loop, meeting CL/CD, wing loading and stall requirements within structural and geometric constraints
- **HORYZN  — Kolibri eVTOL — Aerodynamics Module Development** / `cmsm3thgg0010vj6y72uhz100` — Worked intensively within the RCE-based multidisciplinary design loop alongside aerodynamics, structures, geometry, wing sizing and propulsion sub-teams to achieve design convergence for Kolibri. Ensured aerodynamics module outputs were compatible with and correctly consumed by adjacent modules. - **Middle East Technical University — Numerical Methods for Aerospace Engineers ** / `cmsptn4yc0006vje5wwbmcvw4` — Developed a 2D finite volume solver in FORTRAN to compute potential flow over a NACA 0012 airfoil and a circle at multiple angles of attack. → Converged potential flow solutions obtained for both geometries; velocity vector and streamline distributions validated against expected inviscid flow behaviour at all three angles of attack.
- **Middle East Technical University — Numerical Methods for Aerospace Engineers ** / `cmsptolqq0008vje58rjlmt8v` — Implemented and compared explicit and implicit 1D finite difference schemes for an unsteady heat diffusion equation (parabolic PDE) and a linear convection equation (hyperbolic PDE). → Confirmed FTCS stability limit (d < 0.5) for heat diffusion; demonstrated unconditional stability of BTCS; identified numerical diffusion in FTBS for the wave equation. Results consistent with theoretical Von Neumann stability analysis.
- **Middle East Technical University — Numerical Methods for Aerospace Engineers ** / `cmsptpyva000avje5f2ngf1ks` — Implemented FTCS and DuFort-Frankel schemes to solve the 2D unsteady convection-diffusion equation over a rectangular domain, with parametric studies on grid resolution, convection velocity, and initial condition geometry → Demonstrated conditional stability of FTCS and improved stability characteristics of DuFort-Frankel; confirmed numerical diffusion effects under backward convective differencing; parametric cases validated solver response to varying flow and grid conditions.
- **Middle East Technical University — Finite Element Modelling of a Composite Cantilever Beam** / `cmspu55yo000fvje5vsg3rzev` — Performed static 3D FEA of a 5-layer composite cantilever beam (3 aluminium + 2 glass fibre/epoxy, 500mm × 50mm) under a 5N transverse tip load to determine tip deflection and full stress distribution across all layers. → Maximum tip deflection δ_max = 31.387mm. Dominant stress component identified as σ_x (max ±0.45×10⁸ N/m²) concentrated at the cantilevered root on the top and bottom surfaces. XZ shear stress dominant across the beam body (avg 58,374 N/m²). Results consistent with classical cantilever beam theory.
- **Middle East Technical University — Finite Element Modelling of a Composite Cantilever Beam** / `cmspu6fn3000hvje58n5i6sc5` — Performed multi-analysis 3D FEA of a 3-layer composite cantilever beam (1 aluminium core + 2 glass fibre/epoxy, 200mm × 40mm) under static and harmonic torsional moment loading (Mx = 1Nm), including modal frequency extraction and harmonic response analysis. → Static: δ_max = 2.296mm, θ_max = 5.74×10⁻⁴ rad/mm. First three torsional frequencies identified at 288.275Hz (mode 3), 894.912Hz (mode 6), and 1586.38Hz (mode 8). Harmonic at 1Hz matched static results; harmonic at 144Hz showed dynamic amplification (δ_max = 2.923mm, θ_max = 7.3075×10⁻⁴ rad/mm). Tip displacement confirmed to vary linearly across beam width in all cases
- **Middle East Technical University — Turbofan Compressor Performance Analysis** / `cmspuei9z000lvje5i6aiki9v` — Conducted parametric analysis of turbofan compressor performance by modelling the effects of turbine inlet temperature, operating altitude, and turbine inlet area on corrected mass flow rate and compressor pressure ratio → Demonstrated that increasing T_t4 raises compressor pressure ratio for a given mass flow rate; confirmed θ_0 reduction with altitude and its attenuation near tropopause; showed that increasing A_4 beyond choked-flow area reduces turbine energy extraction and consequently drops compressor mass flow rate. All results presented as parametric families of curves against compressor map limits.
- **Middle East Technical University — Finite Difference Method Solvers for Aerodynamic PDEs** / `cmspulziq000pvje59nizuskl` — Led development of four MATLAB finite difference solvers covering elliptic, parabolic, and hyperbolic PDE classes, applying ASE301 numerical methods experience to architect the solution approach and implement stability-verified codes. → All four solvers delivered converged results within stability limits. Laplace solution reached steady-state at iteration 1,451. Heat equation confirmed theoretical λ < 0.5 stability bound with divergence visually demonstrated at λ = 1. Both wave equation schemes produced clean sinusoidal propagation at ν ≤ 1 and catastrophic divergence at ν = 1.33. MacCormack showed reduced numerical diffusion compared to Lax-Wendroff at equivalent ν values.

- **Middle East Technical University — Numerical Methods for Aerospace Engineers ** / `cmsptolqq0008vje58rjlmt8v` — Implemented and compared explicit and implicit 1D finite difference schemes for an unsteady heat diffusion equation (parabolic PDE) and a linear convection equation (hyperbolic PDE). → Confirmed FTCS stability limit (d < 0.5) for heat diffusion; demonstrated unconditional stability of BTCS; identified numerical diffusion in FTBS for the wave equation. Results consistent with theoretical Von Neumann stability analysis.
- **Middle East Technical University — Numerical Methods for Aerospace Engineers ** / `cmsptpyva000avje5f2ngf1ks` — Implemented FTCS and DuFort-Frankel schemes to solve the 2D unsteady convection-diffusion equation over a rectangular domain, with parametric studies on grid resolution, convection velocity, and initial condition geometry → Demonstrated conditional stability of FTCS and improved stability characteristics of DuFort-Frankel; confirmed numerical diffusion effects under backward convective differencing; parametric cases validated solver response to varying flow and grid conditions.
- **Middle East Technical University — Finite Element Modelling of a Composite Cantilever Beam** / `cmspu55yo000fvje5vsg3rzev` — Performed static 3D FEA of a 5-layer composite cantilever beam (3 aluminium + 2 glass fibre/epoxy, 500mm × 50mm) under a 5N transverse tip load to determine tip deflection and full stress distribution across all layers. → Maximum tip deflection δ_max = 31.387mm. Dominant stress component identified as σ_x (max ±0.45×10⁸ N/m²) concentrated at the cantilevered root on the top and bottom surfaces. XZ shear stress dominant across the beam body (avg 58,374 N/m²). Results consistent with classical cantilever beam theory.
- **Middle East Technical University — Finite Element Modelling of a Composite Cantilever Beam** / `cmspu6fn3000hvje58n5i6sc5` — Performed multi-analysis 3D FEA of a 3-layer composite cantilever beam (1 aluminium core + 2 glass fibre/epoxy, 200mm × 40mm) under static and harmonic torsional moment loading (Mx = 1Nm), including modal frequency extraction and harmonic response analysis. → Static: δ_max = 2.296mm, θ_max = 5.74×10⁻⁴ rad/mm. First three torsional frequencies identified at 288.275Hz (mode 3), 894.912Hz (mode 6), and 1586.38Hz (mode 8). Harmonic at 1Hz matched static results; harmonic at 144Hz showed dynamic amplification (δ_max = 2.923mm, θ_max = 7.3075×10⁻⁴ rad/mm). Tip displacement confirmed to vary linearly across beam width in all cases
- **Middle East Technical University — Finite Difference Method Solvers for Aerodynamic PDEs** / `cmspulziq000pvje59nizuskl` — Led development of four MATLAB finite difference solvers covering elliptic, parabolic, and hyperbolic PDE classes, applying ASE301 numerical methods experience to architect the solution approach and implement stability-verified codes. → All four solvers delivered converged results within stability limits. Laplace solution reached steady-state at iteration 1,451. Heat equation confirmed theoretical λ < 0.5 stability bound with divergence visually demonstrated at λ = 1. Both wave equation schemes produced clean sinusoidal propagation at ν ≤ 1 and catastrophic divergence at ν = 1.33. MacCormack showed reduced numerical diffusion compared to Lax-Wendroff at equivalent ν values.

### Software / Digital Systems
- **HORYZN  — Kolibri eVTOL — Aerodynamics Module Development** / `cmsm3ixrw000svj6yk0weq016` — Participated in structured handover sessions with the outgoing Mission Pulse Phase 1 team to understand the design, shortcomings and failure modes of the Frankenstein prototype. Analysed the previous CPACS files, MATLAB code and PAWAT outputs to reconstruct what had been built and why transition had failed. Collaborated across the full Design Loop team to document lessons learned and define requirements list for the Phase 2 prototype — Kolibri. Requirements captured and managed in Notion. → Comprehensive requirements list established for Kolibri Phase 2, identifying transition failure as the primary design constraint to resolve
- **HORYZN  — Kolibri eVTOL — Aerodynamics Module Development** / `cmsm3lgib000uvj6yzkjd47i1` — Led the aerodynamics team's contribution to the Kolibri configuration selection process. Identified the root cause of Frankenstein's transition failure as wingtip-mounted propellers creating adverse interference at the 25kg MTOW scale. Proposed a Cessna-type fixed-wing configuration with a front tractor propeller and lift motors mounted on the wing, supported by case studies from undergraduate aircraft design projects and CFD analysis in OpenFOAM demonstrating adequate glide performance during transition in the event of propulsion switching delays. Presented the configuration argument across multiple design reviews, convincing the team and securing agreement to proceed. → Cessna-type fixed-wing hybrid lift-and-cruise configuration adopted for Kolibri, directly resolving the transition failure mode of Frankenstein
- **HORYZN  — Kolibri eVTOL — Aerodynamics Module Development** / `cmsm3npre000wvj6yr6elrnaw` — Developed and maintained the aerodynamics module within the HORYZN multidisciplinary design loop. The module interfaced with CPACS via TiGL and TiXi libraries, using PAWAT as the core aerodynamic solver within a MATLAB class-based architecture. Implemented methods covering airfoil database management, CPACS geometry parsing, aerodynamic mapping across Reynolds number and angle of attack ranges, twist optimisation, stall velocity and force calculations, neutral point calculation and results writing back to CPACS. Wrote full module documentation including README, UML class diagram, function descriptions and I/O specifications. Managed module codebase on Git. → Fully documented functional aerodynamics module integrated into the HORYZN RCE design loop, delivering aerodynamic coefficients, forces, moments and aeromap data to downstream modules via CPACS
- **HORYZN  — Kolibri eVTOL — Aerodynamics Module Development** / `cmsm3qzd2000yvj6ya3kpt1f7` — Participated in the aerodynamics team's airfoil selection process for Kolibri's lifting surfaces. Fed selected airfoil profiles into the CPACS aircraft definition and ran iterative optimisation of wing geometry including twist distribution, span, chord and sweep through the aerodynamics module. Coordinated with structures and geometry sub-teams within the design loop to ensure aerodynamic outputs were compatible with structural and sizing constraints. → Optimised lifting surface definition for Kolibri delivered to the design loop, meeting CL/CD, wing loading and stall requirements within structural and geometric constraints
- **HORYZN  — Kolibri eVTOL — Aerodynamics Module Development** / `cmsm3thgg0010vj6y72uhz100` — Worked intensively within the RCE-based multidisciplinary design loop alongside aerodynamics, structures, geometry, wing sizing and propulsion sub-teams to achieve design convergence for Kolibri. - **Technical University of Munich — Applied CFD Channel & Cavity Flow Simulation** / `cmsou9cux0033vjwbjw1rqgkm` — Modeled 2D/3D dual-cavity channel geometries, built multi-block structured grids, and executed comparative subsonic CFD simulations across 5 test cases in ANSYS ICEM CFD and ANSYS CFX → Proved that the non-algebraic SSG Reynolds Stress Model accurately captured flow separation and recirculation vortices inside deep cavities where SST failed, and demonstrated that 2nd-order advection schemes eliminated artificial numerical diffusion present in 1st-order upwind calculations
- **Technical University of Munich — Simulation of the Flowfield of a Multirotor in Axial Flight** / `cmsouotod0037vjwb7fap95rw` — OpenFOAM Multirotor CFD Mesh Generation & Parallel Domain Setup → Generated a high-quality multi-region 3D mesh with fine cell resolution localized around all 4 rotor disks, enabling stable parallel solver execution in simpleFoam.
- **Technical University of Munich — Simulation of the Flowfield of a Multirotor in Axial Flight** / `cmsouqttl0039vjwbl97g7inh` — BEMT Actuator Disk Source Integration & Axial Flow Field Simulation → Calculated individual rotor effective lift (1061 N to 1144 N) and drag torque (-101 Nm to -110 Nm), validated axial pressure jump profiles against analytical momentum theory, and demonstrated that rotor tip vortices do not interact when inter-rotor spacing equals or exceeds disk diameter.
- **Technical University of Munich — Geospatial Analysis & Cartography — Nakuru County Geotourism Research** / `cmsq1c9jb002hvje5gujnnk56` — Formulated the spatial data processing pipeline and generated all regional GIS figures, geosite localizations, and geological mapping overlays. → Completed 100% of the cartographic and spatial analysis deliverables required for the research publication
- **Middle East Technical University — Numerical Methods for Aerospace Engineers ** / `cmsptn4yc0006vje5wwbmcvw4` — Developed a 2D finite volume solver in FORTRAN to compute potential flow over a NACA 0012 airfoil and a circle at multiple angles of attack. → Converged potential flow solutions obtained for both geometries; velocity vector and streamline distributions validated against expected inviscid flow behaviour at all three angles of attack.
- **Middle East Technical University — Numerical Methods for Aerospace Engineers ** / `cmsptolqq0008vje58rjlmt8v` — Implemented and compared explicit and implicit 1D finite difference schemes for an unsteady heat diffusion equation (parabolic PDE) and a linear convection equation (hyperbolic PDE). → Confirmed FTCS stability limit (d < 0.5) for heat diffusion; demonstrated unconditional stability of BTCS; identified numerical diffusion in FTBS for the wave equation. Results consistent with theoretical Von Neumann stability analysis.
- **Middle East Technical University — Numerical Methods for Aerospace Engineers ** / `cmsptpyva000avje5f2ngf1ks` — Implemented FTCS and DuFort-Frankel schemes to solve the 2D unsteady convection-diffusion equation over a rectangular domain, with parametric studies on grid resolution, convection velocity, and initial condition geometry → Demonstrated conditional stability of FTCS and improved stability characteristics of DuFort-Frankel; confirmed numerical diffusion effects under backward convective differencing; parametric cases validated solver response to varying flow and grid conditions.
- **Middle East Technical University — Finite Element Modelling of a Composite Cantilever Beam** / `cmspu55yo000fvje5vsg3rzev` — Performed static 3D FEA of a 5-layer composite cantilever beam (3 aluminium + 2 glass fibre/epoxy, 500mm × 50mm) under a 5N transverse tip load to determine tip deflection and full stress distribution across all layers. → Maximum tip deflection δ_max = 31.387mm. Dominant stress component identified as σ_x (max ±0.45×10⁸ N/m²) concentrated at the cantilevered root on the top and bottom surfaces. XZ shear stress dominant across the beam body (avg 58,374 N/m²). Results consistent with classical cantilever beam theory.
- **Middle East Technical University — Finite Element Modelling of a Composite Cantilever Beam** / `cmspu6fn3000hvje58n5i6sc5` — Performed multi-analysis 3D FEA of a 3-layer composite cantilever beam (1 aluminium core + 2 glass fibre/epoxy, 200mm × 40mm) under static and harmonic torsional moment loading (Mx = 1Nm), including modal frequency extraction and harmonic response analysis. → Static: δ_max = 2.296mm, θ_max = 5.74×10⁻⁴ rad/mm. First three torsional frequencies identified at 288.275Hz (mode 3), 894.912Hz (mode 6), and 1586.38Hz (mode 8). Harmonic at 1Hz matched static results; harmonic at 144Hz showed dynamic amplification (δ_max = 2.923mm, θ_max = 7.3075×10⁻⁴ rad/mm). Tip displacement confirmed to vary linearly across beam width in all cases
- **Middle East Technical University — Turbofan Compressor Performance Analysis** / `cmspuei9z000lvje5i6aiki9v` — Conducted parametric analysis of turbofan compressor performance by modelling the effects of turbine inlet temperature, operating altitude, and turbine inlet area on corrected mass flow rate and compressor pressure ratio → Demonstrated that increasing T_t4 raises compressor pressure ratio for a given mass flow rate; confirmed θ_0 reduction with altitude and its attenuation near tropopause; showed that increasing A_4 beyond choked-flow area reduces turbine energy extraction and consequently drops compressor mass flow rate. All results presented as parametric families of curves against compressor map limits.
- **Middle East Technical University — Finite Difference Method Solvers for Aerodynamic PDEs** / `cmspulziq000pvje59nizuskl` — Led development of four MATLAB finite difference solvers covering elliptic, parabolic, and hyperbolic PDE classes, applying ASE301 numerical methods experience to architect the solution approach and implement stability-verified codes. → All four solvers delivered converged results within stability limits. Laplace solution reached steady-state at iteration 1,451. Heat equation confirmed theoretical λ < 0.5 stability bound with divergence visually demonstrated at λ = 1. Both wave equation schemes produced clean sinusoidal propagation at ν ≤ 1 and catastrophic divergence at ν = 1.33. MacCormack showed reduced numerical diffusion compared to Lax-Wendroff at equivalent ν values.

### Aerodynamics / Simulation
- **HORYZN  — Kolibri eVTOL — Aerodynamics Module Development** / `cmsm3ixrw000svj6yk0weq016` — Participated in structured handover sessions with the outgoing Mission Pulse Phase 1 team to understand the design, shortcomings and failure modes of the Frankenstein prototype. Analysed the previous CPACS files, MATLAB code and PAWAT outputs to reconstruct what had been built and why transition had failed. Collaborated across the full Design Loop team to document lessons learned and define requirements list for the Phase 2 prototype — Kolibri. Requirements captured and managed in Notion. → Comprehensive requirements list established for Kolibri Phase 2, identifying transition failure as the primary design constraint to resolve
- **HORYZN  — Kolibri eVTOL — Aerodynamics Module Development** / `cmsm3lgib000uvj6yzkjd47i1` — Led the aerodynamics team's contribution to the Kolibri configuration selection process. Identified the root cause of Frankenstein's transition failure as wingtip-mounted propellers creating adverse interference at the 25kg MTOW scale. Proposed a Cessna-type fixed-wing configuration with a front tractor propeller and lift motors mounted on the wing, supported by case studies from undergraduate aircraft design projects and CFD analysis in OpenFOAM demonstrating adequate glide performance during transition in the event of propulsion switching delays. Presented the configuration argument across multiple design reviews, convincing the team and securing agreement to proceed. → Cessna-type fixed-wing hybrid lift-and-cruise configuration adopted for Kolibri, directly resolving the transition failure mode of Frankenstein
- **HORYZN  — Kolibri eVTOL — Aerodynamics Module Development** / `cmsm3npre000wvj6yr6elrnaw` — Developed and maintained the aerodynamics module within the HORYZN multidisciplinary design loop. The module interfaced with CPACS via TiGL and TiXi libraries, using PAWAT as the core aerodynamic solver within a MATLAB class-based architecture. Implemented methods covering airfoil database management, CPACS geometry parsing, aerodynamic mapping across Reynolds number and angle of attack ranges, twist optimisation, stall velocity and force calculations, neutral point calculation and results writing back to CPACS. Wrote full module documentation including README, UML class diagram, function descriptions and I/O specifications. Managed module codebase on Git. → Fully documented functional aerodynamics module integrated into the HORYZN RCE design loop, delivering aerodynamic coefficients, forces, moments and aeromap data to downstream modules via CPACS
- **HORYZN  — Kolibri eVTOL — Aerodynamics Module Development** / `cmsm3qzd2000yvj6ya3kpt1f7` — Participated in the aerodynamics team's airfoil selection process for Kolibri's lifting surfaces. Ran systematic case studies across candidate airfoils using XFLR5 and Flow5 to evaluate CL/CD performance across the expected Reynolds number range. Fed selected airfoil profiles into the CPACS aircraft definition and ran iterative optimisation of wing geometry including twist distribution, span, chord and sweep through the aerodynamics module. Coordinated with structures and geometry sub-teams within the design loop to ensure aerodynamic outputs were compatible with structural and sizing constraints. → Optimised lifting surface definition for Kolibri delivered to the design loop, meeting CL/CD, wing loading and stall requirements within structural and geometric constraints
- **HORYZN  — Kolibri eVTOL — Aerodynamics Module Development** / `cmsm3thgg0010vj6y72uhz100` — Worked intensively within the RCE-based multidisciplinary design loop alongside aerodynamics, structures, geometry, wing sizing and propulsion sub-teams to achieve design convergence for Kolibri. Ensured aerodynamics module outputs were compatible with and correctly consumed by adjacent modules. Participated in extended working sessions — frequently 18 to 20 hours — to resolve inter-module conflicts and iterate toward a stable baseline design. Contributed aerodynamics expertise to cross-team configuration discussions and design decisions throughout the convergence process → Converged multidisciplinary baseline design achieved for Kolibri, with aerodynamics module outputs validated against structural, geometry and propulsion module requirements
- **HORYZN  — Kolibri eVTOL — Aerodynamics Module Development** / `cmsm3vhnh0012vj6yw25lh6bk` — Prepared and delivered a comprehensive handover of the aerodynamics module to the incoming team and downstream design teams. Handover package included the full codebase on Git, README documentation, UML class diagram, function descriptions, I/O path specifications, calculation options documentation, Flow5 validation task documentation and a formal handover document. Ensured the receiving team could understand, operate and extend the module without loss of design intent or technical context. → Complete aerodynamics module handed over with full documentation. - **Middle East Technical University — Finite Element Modelling of a Composite Cantilever Beam** / `cmspu55yo000fvje5vsg3rzev` — Performed static 3D FEA of a 5-layer composite cantilever beam (3 aluminium + 2 glass fibre/epoxy, 500mm × 50mm) under a 5N transverse tip load to determine tip deflection and full stress distribution across all layers. → Maximum tip deflection δ_max = 31.387mm. Dominant stress component identified as σ_x (max ±0.45×10⁸ N/m²) concentrated at the cantilevered root on the top and bottom surfaces. XZ shear stress dominant across the beam body (avg 58,374 N/m²). - **Middle East Technical University — Finite Element Modelling of a Composite Cantilever Beam** / `cmspu6fn3000hvje58n5i6sc5` — Performed multi-analysis 3D FEA of a 3-layer composite cantilever beam (1 aluminium core + 2 glass fibre/epoxy, 200mm × 40mm) under static and harmonic torsional moment loading (Mx = 1Nm), including modal frequency extraction and harmonic response analysis. → Static: δ_max = 2.296mm, θ_max = 5.74×10⁻⁴ rad/mm. First three torsional frequencies identified at 288.275Hz (mode 3), 894.912Hz (mode 6), and 1586.38Hz (mode 8). Harmonic at 1Hz matched static results; harmonic at 144Hz showed dynamic amplification (δ_max = 2.923mm, θ_max = 7.3075×10⁻⁴ rad/mm). Tip displacement confirmed to vary linearly across beam width in all cases
- **Middle East Technical University — Turbofan Compressor Performance Analysis** / `cmspuei9z000lvje5i6aiki9v` — Conducted parametric analysis of turbofan compressor performance by modelling the effects of turbine inlet temperature, operating altitude, and turbine inlet area on corrected mass flow rate and compressor pressure ratio → Demonstrated that increasing T_t4 raises compressor pressure ratio for a given mass flow rate; confirmed θ_0 reduction with altitude and its attenuation near tropopause; showed that increasing A_4 beyond choked-flow area reduces turbine energy extraction and consequently drops compressor mass flow rate. All results presented as parametric families of curves against compressor map limits.
- **Middle East Technical University — Finite Difference Method Solvers for Aerodynamic PDEs** / `cmspulziq000pvje59nizuskl` — Led development of four MATLAB finite difference solvers covering elliptic, parabolic, and hyperbolic PDE classes, applying ASE301 numerical methods experience to architect the solution approach and implement stability-verified codes. → All four solvers delivered converged results within stability limits. Laplace solution reached steady-state at iteration 1,451. Heat equation confirmed theoretical λ < 0.5 stability bound with divergence visually demonstrated at λ = 1. Both wave equation schemes produced clean sinusoidal propagation at ν ≤ 1 and catastrophic divergence at ν = 1.33. MacCormack showed reduced numerical diffusion compared to Lax-Wendroff at equivalent ν values.
- **Middle East Technical University — Conceptual Design of a Fixed-Wing Luxury Aircraft (Graduation Project I)** / `cmspw1un6000vvje5kmqndoyb` — Defined customer design requirements and conducted a structured competitor benchmarking study across six business jet aircraft to anchor the conceptual design baseline. → Established verified design requirements and a competitor data matrix covering T/W, W/S, fuel weight, empty weight, range, cruise Mach, and geometric parameters — used as the primary reference baseline throughout all subsequent design phases.
- **Middle East Technical University — Conceptual Design of a Fixed-Wing Luxury Aircraft (Graduation Project I)** / `cmspw333m000xvje53phyb2pb` — Estimated initial take-off gross weight W₀ using mission segment weight fractions and conducted parametric trade studies to characterise W₀ sensitivity to range, endurance, and payload. → Converged initial W₀ = 19,500 kg. Trade study confirmed payload as the dominant driver of gross weight, informing conservative payload assumptions carried forward in refined sizing
- **Middle East Technical University — Conceptual Design of a Fixed-Wing Luxury Aircraft (Graduation Project I)** / `cmspw4nwo000zvje54ytjiic9` — Selected the wing airfoil through comparative aerodynamic analysis of five candidate profiles and sized all primary wing geometry parameters. → Wing geometry fully defined: AR 8.5, sweep 28°, taper 0.3, dihedral 3°. NACA 23112 selected as wing airfoil and carried forward into all subsequent aerodynamic and sizing calculations.
- **Middle East Technical University — Conceptual Design of a Fixed-Wing Luxury Aircraft (Graduation Project I)** / `cmspw5ztr0011vje5u7jgj2qi` — Determined final thrust-to-weight ratio and wing loading through multi-constraint parametric analysis, then refined the take-off gross weight estimate using updated mission segment fractions. → Refined W₀ = 20,600 kg, fuel weight = 8,200 kg. - **Middle East Technical University — Conceptual Design of a Fixed-Wing Luxury Aircraft (Graduation Project I)** / `cmspw7nmt0013vje5zycjwohl` — Sized all major airframe components — fuselage, wing, tail surfaces, and engines — and estimated the aircraft centre of gravity from component weight and moment arm data → Fuselage length 23.4 m, wing area 49 m², wingspan 20.4 m, engine thrust 39 kN/engine (×2), T-tail configuration. CG located at 11.51 m from nose, within acceptable range relative to aerodynamic centre.
- **Middle East Technical University — Conceptual Design of a Fixed-Wing Luxury Aircraft (Graduation Project I)** / `cmspw8yv70015vje56h1vv2a3` — Designed the landing gear configuration, sized tyres and struts, validated geometric placement, and produced the final three-view layout drawing. → Tricycle landing gear fully sized and geometrically validated. - **Middle East Technical University — Conceptual Design of a VTOL Personal Air Vehicle (Graduation Project II)** / `cmspwizpn0019vje5n0oxou6a` — Defined customer design requirements and benchmarked six light civil helicopters to establish the design baseline. → Verified requirements set and competitor data matrix covering TOGW, empty weight fraction, rotor diameter, disk loading, cruise speed, and range — used as baseline throughout all subsequent design phases.
- **Middle East Technical University — Conceptual Design of a VTOL Personal Air Vehicle (Graduation Project II)** / `cmspwkd4f001bvje57slm61b9` — Selected the rotor airfoil and sized the main and tail rotors through Blade Element Momentum Theory analysis across three disk loading cases → Main rotor radius = 5.2 m, tail rotor radius = 0.88 m, FM = 0.72, hover power = 306 kW, root pitch angle = 16.7°, TOGW = 2,014 kg, empty weight fraction = 0.4268.
- **Middle East Technical University — Conceptual Design of a VTOL Personal Air Vehicle (Graduation Project II)** / `cmspwlr6n001dvje51vlxlfbs` — Estimated helicopter component weights iteratively using empirical group equations and selected the engine based on power requirements. → TOGW = 2,014 kg, empty weight = 865 kg, empty weight fraction = 0.4268 — within customer-specified maximum of 0.45. - **Middle East Technical University — Conceptual Design of a VTOL Personal Air Vehicle (Graduation Project II)** / `cmspwmteb001fvje5h5zw0w37` — Computed total power required across the forward flight speed envelope to validate engine selection and characterise helicopter performance. → Power curve validated engine selection at 632 kW with adequate margin at cruise speed of 250 km/h. Confirmed expected power bucket behaviour — induced power dominates at low speed, profile and parasite power dominate at high speed.
- **Middle East Technical University — Conceptual Design of a VTOL Personal Air Vehicle (Graduation Project II)** / `cmspwo3wl001hvje5j108ldhf` — Defined all major design configuration choices and produced the final three-view engineering drawing. → Complete rotorcraft configuration defined and documented. - **Middle East Technical University — Simulation of a piezoelectric d15 torsion sensor** / `cmspy67x7001wvje5npg4n59n` — Built the full Abaqus CAE finite element model of the cantilevered piezoelectric beam including geometry, boundary conditions, constraints, and mesh. → Fully meshed and constrained Abaqus CAE model with correct piezoelectric element type, material orientations, and boundary conditions — ready for simulation
- **Middle East Technical University — Simulation of a piezoelectric d15 torsion sensor** / `cmspy7uew001yvje5qkfr07ms` — Executed three sequential FEA analyses — quasi-static, modal, and harmonic — to extract sensor voltages and electromechanical coupling coefficients across torsional modes. → Quasi-static sensor voltage = 1.789 V at 1 Hz. - **Amazilia Aerospace GmbH — Aircraft Battery Charging Unit (ABCU)** / `cmslym8dk0003vjtk9jmmadj6` — Following a polarity reversal incident during field testing of a Pipistrel aircraft that caused a short circuit and wire damage, received brief from CTO to design a safe standalone aircraft battery charging unit. Researched LiFePO4 battery chemistry, charging characteristics and available charging system suppliers to establish a technically grounded requirements baseline. → Clear requirements baseline established covering dual-voltage capability (12V and 24V LiFePO4), polarity protection, voltage indication, field deployability and safe external charging operation
- **Amazilia Aerospace GmbH — Aircraft Battery Charging Unit (ABCU)** / `cmslynjpz0005vjtko4xpmzxv` — Developed system architecture for the ABCU covering power input, charging system selection, voltage switching between 12V and 24V LiFePO4 configurations, polarity protection, battery voltage indication and enclosure design. Conducted detailed component market study matching available charging systems against Amazilia's battery specifications. Selected components capable of handling both 12V and 24V LiFePO4 chemistries with switchable output, allowing field teams to switch between configurations during operations. Optimised component selection for cost following design review feedback. → Approved system architecture and component selection covering dual-voltage charging capability with polarity protection and field-deployable form factor

## 6. 6A observations — not 6B decisions

- The database contains strong evidence clusters around systems engineering, aircraft systems, systems integration, UAS/eVTOL, software/digital systems, verification/testing, and R&D. The individual records—not activity counts—are the evidence.
- Kipepeo contains both technical systems/R&D evidence and venture-building evidence. These should remain distinguishable in 6B rather than allowing corporate/venture activities to crowd out engineering evidence.
- TUM, METU, HORYZN and Amazilia provide distinct academic, experimental and professional evidence. The map preserves those distinctions rather than merging them into a single narrative.
- The database includes activities whose strongest eventual use may be teaching, research, licences/certifications, or supporting context rather than the core Professional Experience section.
- No final inclusion/exclusion, bullet count, ordering, or wording decision is made here. Those are Phase 6B decisions.

## 7. Traceability

Every mapped project and activity retains its database ID. The next stage should use these IDs when making 6B selection decisions so that every Master CV entry remains traceable to the career database.
